Fresh inline actual Lists use their allocation-owned element projection while exposed aliases and unknown source values still fail closed. agent-source-domains.property compares generated admission partitions, typed/erased native execution, identical nested Script text, native-host preparation and one deep import graph with independent Node; agent_source_domains checks typed CLI behavior in both memory modes. The original agent property now passes. The separately reproduced supplied eval-record regression is repaired by the captured Annex B Reference and corrected admission assertions described above; the reproduced eval-source-flow admission regression is repaired by the Array join source model described below. The separately reproduced pinned Atomics.wait helper admission gap is repaired by the effect-origin graph described above. Existing CType, declarations and runtime agent operations suffice. Host eligibility, exact reciprocal source/agent clause evidence and exhaustive ECMAScript 2026 coverage remain open.

Generated mutable storage now lowers from one typed declaration collection into a retained semantic-agent block. Module instantiation/evaluation guards, binding/root/TDZ cells, environments, namespace caches, CommonJS records, class-name/computed/decorator cells and callable/event identity caches use that common projection. Immutable C literal/template data remains shared. A keyed runtime storage worklist preserves the same block across Realms and legacy dispatch contexts while independent agents receive distinct blocks; retirement removes their registry roots. The reproduced native worker Module value/metadata leak (0:0 then 1:1 versus independent Node fresh 0:0) is repaired. agent-module-storage.property compares retired/concurrent workers, cached imports, live export cells, metadata, class/CJS state and one retained Module graph with Node in typed/erased inputs and both memory modes. Runtime-agent properties also verify same-thread agent switching, shared dispatch identity and retirement. agent_class_name_storage provides typed CLI ownership coverage. The separately reproduced finite-agent-source admission gap is repaired by the execution-domain worklist described above. The separately reproduced serial-dispatch owner diagnostic is repaired by the ambient namespace classification described above; libdispatch is unavailable here. Native pthread carrier sharing is verified independently. Module/Realm/agent eligibility, exact reciprocal storage evidence and exhaustive target coverage remain open.

The import.meta host-hook review selects the pinned default empty property List and default unused finalization. Exact HostGetImportMetaProperties and HostFinalizeImportMeta rows now reference the retained Module graph property, one representative native reference-collection stress, typed default-host CLI behavior, declaration and runtime anchor through reciprocal registry entries. This records those choices without promoting the broader ImportMeta/NewTarget parent, the import.meta feature map, Module/Realm/agent eligibility or signed exhaustive coverage.

import.meta now lazily creates and retains one ordinary null-prototype object on its canonical Module Record. The native host uses the pinned default empty HostGetImportMetaProperties List and unused HostFinalizeImportMeta result. Static imports, retained callable reads, repeated expressions and rejected evaluation keep that identity; different Module Records keep distinct objects. The source-value graph caches the corresponding owner by SourceFile for finite source selection. One existing source-tree early-error worklist now rejects ImportMeta under Script, eval and Function source goals, including nested bodies. An empty ImportMeta declaration admits typed source without inventing host properties. One offset-preserving token projection restores MetaProperty nodes and checker metadata before binding, including unparenthesized new callees, while keeping regex/template text intact. Literal meta/target spelling is checked by the source-tree worklist. Retained Module function declarations seed the existing reachability graph so uninvoked callable bindings keep their native definition. import-meta-record.property compares ordinary shape/descriptors, mutation, frozen objects, cached rejection and one deep retained Module graph with independent Node in typed/erased input and both memory modes; import_meta_record checks typed imports and SyntaxError boundaries. The unsupported MetaProperty regression is reproduced on untouched commit 753c14399. Host property extensions, exact choice/clause review, Module/Realm/agent eligibility and exhaustive target coverage remain open.

The obsolete async captured-declaration compile-failure fixture now asserts native execution after suspension. The renamed async_cfg_captured_declaration case prints the captured value agreed with independent Node. The existing canonical CFG property retains fail-closed coverage for an unrepresented local class suspension tree. This updates regression expectations for the existing shared closure/frame implementation; no language or host eligibility claim is promoted.

Imported host sources now retain their CommonJS or ECMAScript format in the canonical source-unit plan. Explicit ECMAScript roots preserve their parse goal; inferred JavaScript imports respect extension, nearest package type and represented module syntax. CommonJS wrappers execute through the retained module record rather than the old export-shape classifier. Source environments initialize immutable import cells from evaluated host exports. Module top-level this also resolves to undefined through the ordinary CLI. Module C names now encode the complete relative path component List, including extensions, using the shared reversible UTF-16 encoder; the lossy basename and punctuation normalizer is deleted. The source-unit-format property compares wrapper identity, package/extension/syntax partitions and one retained dependency graph with independent Node in typed/erased inputs and both memory modes. The typed source_unit_format case adds same-basename extension ownership and direct eval imports; the existing wrapped CommonJS callable regression passes. Complete Node format detection/interoperability, arbitrary package support, eligible host hooks and exhaustive target evidence remain open.

Finite source analysis now follows one alias graph while retaining the import binding's value/namespace mode. JSON default data, anonymous default expressions and ordinary Module exports feed the existing source cells; namespace owners are cached by SourceFile and populated through a worklist, including cycles and live exporter cells. This replaces the synthetic-checker-target fallback to a same-spelled global. Known imported strings now enter the common eval/Function AOT source closure without a manifest or unsafe gate. JSON __proto__ members remain own data properties in source facts. `module-source-facts.property` compares JSON values, property/array reads, defaults, reexports, namespace cycles, live mutable exports and one retained namespace-path graph with independent Node in typed/erased inputs and both memory modes. Unknown namespace sources keep the stable finite-source diagnostic. `module_source_closure` supplies typed CLI coverage. Eligible module/Realm/agent review and exhaustive target coverage remain open.

Import Reference classification now checks the canonical collection of local checker declarations and projected targets before selecting a Realm-global binding. This preserves ordinary import syntax when an alias resolves to a synthetic JSON SourceFile, an ambient host export or a declaration in another module. The shared classifier replaces the alias-target-only global and unshadowed-intrinsic decisions; no JSON-specific global lookup selector is added. The reproduced JSON default-import ReferenceError is repaired. The JSON property compares all root Types and global-name shadowing against independent Node execution in both memory modes; the existing module-environment property retains its representative import graph stress. `import_binding_ownership` verifies typed JSON aliases, a custom imported Math owner and namespace identity. Exhaustive module/host eligibility and full ECMAScript coverage remain open.

Source identities now distinguish the physical path and ECMAScript parse goal in one source-unit collection. TypeScript materializes distinct Script and Module records over unchanged input text; import resolution projects the Module unit while Script initialization retains its own global environment. The pinned self-importing Script regression now executes its separate Module record and caches later imports. Source discovery shares one worklist across roots. Source analysis includes selected Script bodies, recognizes Module top-level this, follows statement/expression guards, interns allocation-site callable/object/Promise/generator facts and formal rest locations, and shares one monotone solution between eval and Function source queries. `source-unit-goal.property` compares typed/erased native execution with an independent Node Script/Module loader and one retained source-unit graph stress; `source_unit_goal_import` checks the typed Module cache and live cells. Module/Realm/agent eligibility and exhaustive target evidence remain open. The JSON primitive default-import ReferenceError reproduced on unchanged HEAD is a separate recorded regression.

Asynchronous Module Record ordering now uses the surrounding retained Agent Record, matching the pinned `IncrementModuleAsyncEvaluationCount` operation. The process-global predecessor is deleted. The native runtime-agent property compares independent-agent and shared-agent Realm partitions, evaluates real pending Module Record graphs, checks cached completion and owning-agent wakeup, and retains the same deep agent graph stress through serial/threaded builds and both memory modes. The independent Node module-evaluation property and typed `module_evaluation_await` consumer provide module regressions. Module/Realm/agent host eligibility and exhaustive target coverage remain open.

The standard `tail-call-optimization` feature tag now has a reviewed mapping to the canonical tail-position clause subtree and its call-evaluation operations. The mapping comes from the pinned clause tree and an independent scan of Test262 metadata, covering every metadata anchor for this tag without matching test filenames. Existing source-tree classification, canonical invocation-record properties and the representative native cross-Realm revoked-Proxy probe support verification readiness. Mapping readiness does not close clauses: Proxy feature mapping, eligible host review and exhaustive target evidence remain open.

Timer globals and `timers`/`timers/promises` imports now expose ordinary Realm-owned callable and module objects. A shared callback descriptor collection and the existing Promise operation collection feed the runtime owners; calls use the canonical Reference and complete actual-argument List instead of timer-specific emitter selectors. Named import cells retain their original functions when the default object changes. Scheduler methods check their receiver brand. Independent Node partitions cover identity, name/length, lookup before arguments, reflected/bound/tail calls, cancellation, scheduler receivers and one wide retained callback List through typed and erased JavaScript inputs in both memory modes; `timer_callable_owners` supplies typed CLI coverage. The ambient-owner failures reproduced in async and Array.fromAsync regressions are repaired. Numeric callback handles remain the native ABI; Node Timeout/Immediate objects and full function prototype/constructibility parity remain open. The pinned native tail-call probe remains claim-blocking because its standard feature tag has no reviewed ready clause mapping. Local evidence does not promote host eligibility or establish full ECMAScript coverage.

The selected can-block capability is now reviewed independently of host eligibility. tests/property/test262-can-block.property.test.ts verifies request-to-agent startup before setup, all source modes, ordered wait coercions, waitAsync independence, Realm inheritance, secondary-agent policy, actual wakeup and a retained evaluation stress. Remaining Realm/module/environment work and eligible exhaustive target evidence stay open.

Retained source binding writes now follow the same environment Reference as reads, before static C lexical lowering. The prior immutable-binding regression is repaired; generated write partitions and the retained environment tree remain regression evidence. Eligible exhaustive target evidence remains open.

Native Test262 startup now carries the selected can-block Boolean from the scenario request into the canonical owning agent record before harness or test execution. Missing/null selection preserves the native CLI agent’s blocking default; Realm evaluation inherits its owning agent’s policy and secondary agents keep their independent blocking policy. Script, strict, raw and Module source goals share this startup path.

Job boundaries, shared-memory observations and ordinary/Atomics accesses now release and restore the recursive runtime lock at one agent scheduling checkpoint. This permits independent agents to progress while another agent polls shared words or growing-view length, including within a synchronous loop or continuously replenished Promise queue. The SC word algorithm and waiter mutex ordering remain separate from this scheduler checkpoint.

The module-dependency runtime error-origin mismatch is repaired by retaining the failing Module Record identity through graph rejection. Native observation selects the origin from one source-role collection; setup, entry, dependency and asynchronous completion retain their distinct report roles. Complete module/Realm host eligibility and exhaustive target evidence remain open.

The runner guard permits thread naming and signals within the native executable’s own captured process ID so threaded GC can suspend agents. External process signals, unscoped kill/tkill, unsafe prctl and process execution stay denied; a runner-owned native self-test checks the permitted and rejected partitions.

Named function expression self-bindings use the shared environment representation; focused differential properties cover private identity, strictness, defaults, shadowing and one deep retained chain. This repairs the recursive named callback used by the pinned agent helper. Exhaustive target coverage and host eligibility still require the open work recorded below.

Test262 agents now use one ordinary operation collection and one retained worker/mailbox/report lifecycle. Agent source strings enter the existing value/location/List graph and the same transitive fixed-point AOT source closure, including stored and reflected calls; unknown source remains one stable unsupported diagnostic. Workers run in independent agent contexts and Realms, retain host liveness until leaving, release recursive runtime locks while waiting or sleeping, and report through a synchronized FIFO. Async callback rejections propagate to the parent, and normal/abrupt teardown interrupts sleeping or blocked agents before joining and releasing contexts. Native Promise callback registration now schedules already-settled records on the owning job queue as well as pending settlements. Broadcast acknowledges retrieval and clones a receiving-Realm SharedArrayBuffer wrapper over the same private storage block. Current growable length is now published on that canonical block, so all clones and tracking views observe growth.

See tests/property/test262-agent.property.test.ts and its independent Node Worker helpers for metadata/role/mutable-callable source closure, generated worker lifecycles, shared clone/Realm/global isolation, actual sync/async wait wakeups and one deep retained report queue. tests/e2e/cases/test262_agents verifies typed stored start/broadcast calls, BigInt delivery and shared growing views. Native context, waiter and shared-storage properties cover affected concurrency, lock release and root cleanup. Global initialization now publishes one retained ordinary owner before walking recursive intrinsic dependencies, eliminating the host-global initialization cycle.

Eligible host review, complete Realm/module fixture behavior, full memory-model and eligible exhaustive target evidence remain open. Agent API implementation and local checks do not automatically promote host capability flags or whole clauses. The full target goal remains unproven.

Runtime agents now use one retained context representation for Realm, parse unit, lexical environment, call/exception/callee state, blocking policy, callable identity registry and next-tick/microtask/immediate/timer queues. Existing queue algorithms operate on the current agent's state; the process-global queue and callable-registry predecessors and thread-local execution-slot predecessors are removed. Independent agents keep their jobs local, while existing dispatch workers retain main-agent delivery and callable identity. Waiter completion is keyed by agent identity across context switches, and retirement unlinks pending/ready waiters and releases owned roots.

See tests/property/runtime-agent.property.test.ts for generated queue/context partitions, actual concurrent independent-agent execution in threaded builds, direct call/exception/callee restoration, owning-agent Promise completion, retired Realm/storage/Promise root release and one deep retained agent graph. Existing waiter, timer, callable-identity and cross-Realm properties and typed atomics_waiters consumers provide affected regressions. This is the runtime isolation foundation: eligible host review, complete can-block scenario selection and complete Realm/memory-model/exhaustive eligible target evidence remain open. No host capability is promoted by these local checks.

Atomics wait, waitAsync and notify now share one retained FIFO waiter representation keyed by private shared block and byte offset. Ordered private-view validation and conversion feed the same sequentially consistent read/enqueue transition; one terminal transition unlinks records for notification or timeout. Blocking releases and restores runtime locks, asynchronous completion runs on the owning agent in the captured Realm, and finite deadlines retain their mathematical value without keeping the process alive. Completed records and cancelled timer slots release storage and Promise roots. The Test262 main agent honors the selected policy and preserves its native default when no selection is supplied.

See tests/property/atomics-waiter-owner.property.test.ts for method metadata, waitable brands, captured index length, conversion/error order, sync policy, shared-location FIFO, deadline and Promise ownership partitions, generated queue prefixes and one deep retained waiter graph. Node differences for notify after non-shared resize and waitAsync after index-triggered growth are checked separately against the pinned private-length algorithms. tests/property/atomics-waiter-storage.property.test.ts verifies actual blocking, foreign-thread wakeup, mixed sync/async location queues and terminal root release; tests/e2e/cases/atomics_waiters verifies typed stored calls across suspension.

Complete host transfer integration, complete Realm and memory-model conformance, partial trailing-word revalidation and eligible exhaustive target evidence remain open. Local waiter evidence does not promote whole clauses or host eligibility.

Atomics word methods now use one operation collection, one captured integer-view record and one cardinality-independent compare-and-exchange lowering. Ordinary per-Realm Atomics owners preserve method metadata, mutable/stored callables and complete argument Lists. Validation captures private length before index conversion; Number/BigInt conversions run in order before fresh view revalidation, store returns the converted value, and compareExchange compares modular raw bits. The existing TypedArray format collection supplies widths and content kinds, including the Buffer byte-owner adapter.

Shared scalar and byte accesses now use compatible native atomic loads/stores. Aligned words preserve complete values, unaligned DataView consumers use atomic byte accesses, and Atomics uses sequential consistency. The per-block scalar mutex predecessor is removed; native 4-byte lock freedom is enforced at compilation. See tests/property/atomics-word-owner.property.test.ts for Node ownership/format/index/coercion/mutation/modular partitions, selected pinned fractional isLockFree and tracking-view revalidation assertions, generated lengths and one deep retained atomic-owner graph; tests/property/atomics-word-storage.property.test.ts verifies contended updates, old values and sequential consistency. tests/e2e/cases/atomics_words covers typed stored calls across suspension.

Complete Realm and memory-model conformance and eligible exhaustive target evidence remain open. The pinned RevalidateAtomicAccess text only checks an element byte start; a partial trailing element after conversion currently fails closed with the stable RangeError diagnostic rather than reading outside native storage, and that endpoint remains an explicit coverage gap. Atomics.pause is absent from the pinned target and is excluded with other post-target proposals. Local word-method evidence does not promote full structured-data or memory-model clauses.

SharedArrayBuffer now uses the same ordinary per-Realm buffer owner builder and operation collection as ArrayBuffer, with explicit slot-kind validation. One retained shared block reserves its maximum extent at a stable address, publishes current length through a sequentially consistent compare-and-exchange loop, and synchronizes scalar accesses through the shared byte-storage boundary. DataView and TypedArray witnesses read the published private length, preserve fixed versus tracking extents, and share live growing storage. Shared species slice rejects non-shared, undersized and same-block results. TypedArray copy/set/slice consumers use synchronized raw accesses; set snapshots only aliased backing blocks and preserves live reads for distinct blocks, and fixed views over growable shared buffers retain the specified integrity behavior.

See tests/property/shared-array-buffer-owner.property.test.ts for Node constructor/metadata/brand/options/species/growing-view/conversion partitions, generated storage lengths and one deep retained view graph; tests/property/shared-array-buffer-storage.property.test.ts separately verifies racing growth, scalar reads without tearing, deterministic competing-write ordering for distinct versus aliased set sources, and mapping release after the final view is dropped. tests/e2e/cases/shared_array_buffer verifies typed stored growth across suspension. Agent execution, shared buffer cloning/host transfer integration, complete Realm behavior and eligible exhaustive target conformance remain open. These local checks do not promote the full structured-data or memory-model clauses.

Array and TypedArray stringification now share one rooted captured-range kernel with live indexed Reads, separator conversion and ordinary element locale invocation. TypedArray join/toLocaleString validate current private view slots and capture their internal length; generic Array consumers still use LengthOfArrayLike. The TypedArray toString property is the actual Array.prototype.toString callable and therefore keeps live join lookup, raw return forwarding and Object-tag fallback. The duplicated Array locale loop and Array-only join loop predecessors are removed. The selected non-ECMA-402 target invokes locale elements without arguments and ignores reserved options.

See tests/property/typed-array-string.property.test.ts for Node metadata/brand/alias/separator/conversion/mutation/abrupt partitions, Array regressions, generated lengths and one deep retained method/view graph, with separate selected non-402 invocation/decimal fallback assertions where Node's Intl behavior differs, and tests/e2e/cases/typed_array_string for typed stored/suspended consumers. Full TypedArray/Realm/JSON/integrity/host coverage, SharedArrayBuffer/Atomics, Buffer backing integration and eligible exhaustive target conformance remain open; local method evidence does not promote a full indexed-collections clause.

TypedArray.sort/toSorted now consume one captured dense value List through the same retained stable sorter as Array.sort/toSorted. Comparison mode selects lexicographic Array defaults or numeric/BigInt TypedArray defaults, including NaN placement and signed-zero order; user comparators keep ordinary undefined-this calls and result conversion. Comparator validation precedes view validation, input values are captured before callbacks, writeback uses fresh integer-indexed validity, and toSorted creates an intrinsic same-element owner without species. Array default string conversions now occur left-to-right with retained strings. The private Array-only sorting entry point is removed.

See tests/property/typed-array-sort.property.test.ts for Node metadata/brand/default/comparator/stability/abrupt/storage-mutation partitions, Array regression partitions, compact generated lengths and one deep retained sort-result graph, and tests/e2e/cases/typed_array_sort for typed numeric/BigInt/derived consumers and suspended stored methods. Remaining TypedArray string algorithms, SharedArrayBuffer/Atomics, Buffer backing integration, complete Realm behavior and eligible exhaustive target conformance remain open; no full indexed-collections clause is promoted.

TypedArray copy/mutation methods now use the shared method and element-format collections, retained storage witnesses and cardinality-independent element/byte loops. CopyWithin coerces target/start/end in order and copies the live applicable prefix with overlap-safe byte movement; fill converts once then refreshes bounds. Set distinguishes typed snapshots from ordered array-like Gets and keeps conversion-before-validity on every element. Slice preserves raw encodings and normative forward-byte effects for aliased species; subarray passes an ordinary argument List retaining tracking/fixed view semantics. ToReversed/with use the intrinsic element kind and ignore species, with value conversion before fresh index validation. Species creation now consumes one general actual List instead of the length-only predecessor.

See tests/property/typed-array-copy.property.test.ts for Node brand/metadata/coercion/overlap/raw-byte/species/storage-mutation partitions, compact generated lengths and one deep retained shared-view graph, and tests/e2e/cases/typed_array_copy for typed stored methods and suspended consumers. Remaining TypedArray sort/string algorithms, SharedArrayBuffer/Atomics, Buffer backing integration, complete Realm behavior and eligible exhaustive target conformance remain open; no full indexed-collections clause is promoted.

TypedArray search and callback traversals now share one method collection and one captured-range algorithm over live integer-indexed Reads. Direction, membership/equality, short-circuiting and reduction state are operation metadata; arbitrary callback values and accumulators remain rooted. Map constructs its species result before callbacks, while filter retains selected values and constructs afterwards; both use shared typed result/bounds/content validation. Source length shadows do not affect traversal, and resize/detach during coercion or callbacks changes live indexed reads without changing the captured range.

See tests/property/typed-array-traversal.property.test.ts for Node metadata/brand/search/callback/order/species/abrupt/storage-mutation partitions, compact generated lengths and one deep retained view/accumulator graph, with a separate pinned-spec content-type ordering assertion where Node differs, and tests/e2e/cases/typed_array_traversal for inferred typed callbacks and suspended stored methods. Remaining TypedArray mutation/copy/sort/string algorithms, SharedArrayBuffer/Atomics, Buffer backing integration, complete Realm behavior and eligible exhaustive target conformance remain open; no full indexed-collections clause is promoted.

TypedArray.from/of now share one factory operation collection, actual item Lists and a retained constructor-result validator. Iterable sources are fully consumed before construction/mapping; array-like sources capture LengthOfArrayLike, construct first and then Get/map/store each element. Constructor and mapper validation precede source access, ordinary strict Set preserves coercion and fresh storage validity, and constructor results are checked for private view slots, current bounds and sufficient internal length. Shared inherited methods remain mutable ordinary values. The ArrayLike declaration and mapper overload preserve callback inference.

See tests/property/typed-array-factory.property.test.ts for Node constructor/result/mapper/iterator/order/mutation/abrupt partitions and one deep retained factory-result graph, and tests/e2e/cases/typed_array_factories for fully typed inferred callbacks, derived owners and suspension. Full TypedArray prototype algorithms, SharedArrayBuffer/Atomics, Buffer backing integration, complete Realm behavior and eligible exhaustive target conformance remain open; this scoped factory implementation does not promote a full clause.

TypedArray constructors now use one element-format collection and one retained ArrayBuffer view record for numeric, clamped, floating and BigInt storage. Construction distinguishes length, ArrayBuffer, typed-view, iterable and array-like sources with actual newTarget prototypes; same-format copies preserve raw bytes. Shared integer-indexed hooks and iterator steps use current fixed/length-tracking witnesses, including resize, out-of-bounds recovery and detachment. Numeric conversion finishes before fresh write validation. DataView and TypedArray share scalar conversion kernels; the Buffer prototype now inherits the actual Uint8Array intrinsic instead of a synthetic hierarchy.

See tests/property/typed-array-owner.property.test.ts for independent Node metadata/constructor/key/storage/conversion/resize/iterator partitions and one deep retained view graph, with separate pinned-spec constructor-order assertions where Node differs, and tests/e2e/cases/typed_array_ordinary for typed suspended consumers. Full TypedArray prototype/static methods, SharedArrayBuffer/Atomics, Buffer backing ArrayBuffer integration, complete Realm behavior and eligible exhaustive target conformance remain open. The unrepresented Buffer backing getter fails closed with a stable diagnostic; no full structured-data clause is promoted.

Integer-indexed owners now share one canonical numeric-key boundary, live validity/length producer and element get/set operations across ordinary property, descriptor, membership, deletion and key enumeration hooks. Buffer supplies the initial byte-storage adapter. Element conversion precedes a fresh validity check, so subsequent typed-view adapters can observe storage changes after coercion. Alternate receivers now observe the indexed own data descriptor, while invalid numeric indices succeed without creating receiver properties or invoking inherited setters. The Buffer-specific integer-index hook predecessor and direct descriptor/read/write storage projections are deleted.

See tests/property/integer-indexed-owner.property.test.ts for Node Buffer canonical/ordinary key, receiver, descriptor, conversion and Proxy partitions with one deep retained indexed-owner graph, tests/property/buffer-ordinary.property.test.ts for byte-kernel regression evidence, and tests/e2e/cases/integer_indexed_receiver and tests/e2e/cases/buffer_object_methods for typed consumers. This is shared representation groundwork; actual TypedArray constructors/views/methods, ArrayBuffer integration, SharedArrayBuffer/Atomics and full eligible target conformance remain open.

Iterator helper activations now retain the captured next/return method Realm in the same linked worklist. Each intercepted native activation enters that Realm, and every normal or abrupt landing restores the calling method Realm. A cross-realm reentrancy probe independently reproduced the previous wrong TypeError origin; Node reports the child method Realm, while the old native path reported the caller Realm.

See tests/property/iterator-helper-realm.property.test.ts for Node vm comparisons of owner/result/error identity, borrowed next methods, close failures and restoration, with one deep alternating-Realm step/close graph. Existing typed/helper worklist and collector evidence still applies. Complete Realm intrinsic/host/module/agent behavior and exhaustive eligible target coverage remain open; host-$262-createRealm is not enabled by this scoped repair.

RegExp source values now use encoded ordinary owners from literal creation through aliases, stored methods, properties and suspension. The native source CType, boxing/unboxing/coercion branches, empty method dispatcher and RegExp-specific fresh-shape purity predecessors are deleted. Literal construction still uses the canonical intrinsic engine owner even when the public global constructor changes; other construction and mutable method/property access use ordinary References and actual Lists.

See tests/property/regexp-ordinary-source.property.test.ts for independent Node owner, descriptor, mutable/deleted method, literal-intrinsic, derived and receiver partitions with one deep retained method/owner graph, and tests/e2e/cases/regexp_ordinary_source for fully typed stored and suspended consumers. Complete static-source admission, engine/object-model/cross-realm semantics and exhaustive eligible target evidence remain open.

Completed RegExp String Iterators now discard matcher/input payload roots through one completion transition, while their branded owners continue returning done. Non-global successful results complete immediately; thrown RegExpExec operations retain retry behavior. RegExpStringIterator declarations now inherit the shared IteratorObject helper surface.

See tests/property/regexp-iterator-gc.property.test.ts for collector retention/release checks using the canonical runtime state, tests/property/regexp-match-all.property.test.ts for direct Node comparison of lazy/species/result/brand/retry behavior, and tests/e2e/cases/regexp_iterator_helpers for typed map/flatMap consumers. Full RegExp/cross-realm and exhaustive eligible target coverage remain open.

RegExp split now uses one species-aware ordinary protocol algorithm. Symbol.split resolves SpeciesConstructor before flags, constructs a sticky splitter before limit conversion, invokes shared RegExpExec and observes live lastIndex/length/capture properties. One encoded result List retains arbitrary captures, including undefined and objects; Unicode advance and zero-width boundaries use the shared UTF-16 kernels. The native String split shortcut, fixed capture kernel and unused adapter/declarations are deleted. String split with a missing Symbol.split now follows ordinary string coercion.

See tests/property/regexp-split-protocol.property.test.ts for Node semantic partitions, custom species/result/Proxy behavior, coercion and abrupt ordering, Unicode boundaries and one deep retained capture graph, and tests/e2e/cases/regexp_split_protocol for typed direct and custom symbol consumers. The current source also already has ordinary replace and matchAll protocol implementations; the old todo description is stale for those paths. Complete RegExp engine/object-model/cross-realm and exhaustive eligible target evidence remain open.

Lazy Iterator helpers now share one operation collection, retained helper state and linked activation worklist for map/filter/take/drop/flatMap and Iterator.concat. Native helper next/return links execute without recursive C calls; modified methods and Proxy wrappers remain ordinary Calls. Captured iterator records preserve opening order and zero-argument calls, arbitrary callback results, unbounded mathematical indices, drop steps without value reads and precise close/error precedence. Concat captures all source iterator methods at creation and opens sources lazily. Completed helpers release captured records and closures while their branded owners remain usable.

See tests/property/iterator-helper-worklist.property.test.ts for Node differential semantic partitions and one deep mixed helper graph covering both step and close, tests/property/iterator-helper-gc.property.test.ts for collector retention/release evidence, and tests/e2e/cases/iterator_lazy_helpers for typed consumers. Complete cross-realm Iterator behavior, activation through arbitrary user Call graphs, and exhaustive eligible Test262 coverage remain open; local helper evidence does not promote the complete control-abstraction clause.

Iterator now has an ordinary per-realm constructor with derived-only construction and an immutable own prototype. Iterator.from uses the shared rooted direct record, captures next once, preserves existing Iterator identities and wraps other object/string iterators in private-slot owners. Shared wrapper methods forward raw next/return results with zero arguments. Constructor and Symbol.toStringTag accessors share the normative setter algorithm, including receiver checks, own descriptor lookup, property creation and strict Set.

See tests/property/iterator-from-owner.property.test.ts for independent Node metadata, construction, opening, setter/Proxy, raw result and retention partitions, with one deep retained wrapper graph, and tests/e2e/cases/iterator_from_ordinary for typed consumers. Lazy helpers, Iterator.concat, remaining cross-realm behavior and exhaustive eligible Test262 coverage remain open.

Iterator eager consumers now share one operation collection and rooted direct IteratorRecord algorithm for forEach, reduce, some, every, find and toArray. Callback validation closes before reading next; callback throws close with incoming throw precedence, while next/done/value failures mark the record complete and propagate without closing. Predicate early completion closes normally and preserves abrupt return behavior. Encoded item Lists and retained accumulators preserve arbitrary values; the counter remains an unbounded mathematical integer projected to Number for callbacks. The bool-selected predecessor has been removed.

See tests/property/iterator-eager-consumer.property.test.ts for Node differential metadata, direct receiver, callback, close and empty/short-circuit partitions with one deep retained iterator/owner graph, and tests/e2e/cases/iterator_eager_consumers for typed Set iterator consumers. Iterator constructor/from, lazy helpers with a depth-independent activation worklist, remaining iterator metadata/cross-realm behavior and exhaustive eligible Test262 coverage remain open; no full control-abstraction clause is promoted.

TextEncoder/TextDecoder now use per-realm ordinary owners for the represented UTF-8 surface. One member collection installs callable encode/decode functions and encoding/fatal/ignoreBOM getters with Node metadata; actual constructor argument Lists and newTarget prototypes use the existing ordinary path. Cached wrappers retain payloads and prototypes across allocation and suspension. Native source CTypes, constructor dispatch and method/boxing shortcuts have been removed. Receiver validation throws TypeError before conversion.

UTF-8 encode uses the shared well-formed String kernel to replace lone surrogates. Decode walks byte sequences with bounded scalar state, replacement/reconsumption for malformed prefixes and initial BOM removal. Buffer, ArrayBuffer and DataView inputs share native byte borrows and current DataView witnesses after options conversion. See tests/property/text-codec-owner.property.test.ts and tests/e2e/cases/text_codec_ordinary for Node differential owner/byte/argument/mutation partitions, one deep retained codec graph and a fully typed suspended consumer. Complete Encoding labels and options, streaming state, encodeInto, Uint8Array result fidelity and complete util import parity remain open. Unsupported represented options and labels fail closed; these host API checks do not establish ECMAScript conformance.

Environment property and element reads now return an encoded string-or-undefined result from one runtime producer. Prior TypeScript assignments cannot narrow a later absent native variable into an empty string after deletion or host mutation. Native key conversion allocates the complete key instead of truncating it to a fixed buffer. Node differential checks cover typed import aliases and direct global reads, empty/present/absent transitions and long keys in CLI/observation profiles and both memory modes. Named process imports in the JavaScript observation profile and loop-context JavaScript process.env access still fail closed because ordinary module/global owners are missing; the property test preserves those stable diagnostic contracts. See tests/property/process-env-boxed.property.test.ts and tests/e2e/cases/process_env_boxed_read for the typed suspended consumer. Ordinary process/env owners, complete assignment coercion and remaining Node process parity remain open.

Proxy defineProperty success validation now uses one actual target descriptor and extensibility algorithm, replacing duplicated array, function and ordinary-object branches. A successful trap cannot claim to make a non-configurable writable data property non-writable while the actual target remains writable. Nested target proxies preserve descriptor-before-extensibility observation, false traps skip target validation, and abrupt target descriptor operations propagate their original value. See tests/property/proxy-define-descriptor.property.test.ts for Node differential transition/compatibility/order partitions and one deep retained proxy graph.

The function-proxy regression now uses explicit strict functions and actual accessor syntax, checks TypeError identity, and verifies strict rejection of locked prototype assignment against Node. tests/e2e/cases/proxy_function_prototype_invariants passes in both memory modes. Remaining Proxy internal methods, exotic descriptor behavior, cross-realm cases and exhaustive eligible Test262 evidence remain open.

Set composition methods now live on the ordinary Set prototype and share one operation collection, rooted set-like record and insertion-cursor/IteratorRecord algorithm. Validation observes size, its numeric conversion, has and keys in order after receiver branding. Size selects the prescribed live-has or keys-iterator path; copy timing, duplicate keys, SameValueZero, mutations and predicate early closing follow that shared representation. Results use the intrinsic Set prototype without consulting species. The native two-Set kernels and fixed element-type emitter branches have been deleted. ReadonlySetLike declarations accept Map and custom operands.

Node differential properties cover metadata, receiver/operand validation, observable conversions, actual arguments, copy/mutation ordering, duplicate keys and abrupt iterator closing, with one deep retained owner/result graph. See tests/property/set-composition.property.test.ts and tests/e2e/cases/set_composition_set_like. Existing composition and ignored-argument regressions use the same path in both memory modes. Complete collection and iterator clauses, cross-realm behavior and exhaustive eligible Test262 coverage remain open.

ArrayBuffer now has per-realm ordinary constructor/prototype owners from one operation collection. Construction evaluates the complete argument List, converts length and the observable maxByteLength option, and uses the actual newTarget prototype. Resize, species-aware slice, transfer and transferToFixedLength share native storage transitions with ordered conversion, attachment and bounds checks. The native source CType, fixed constructor/property paths and fresh-allocation effect shortcuts have been removed. Ordinary boxed property reads preserve mutable accessor results instead of narrowing them from static declarations.

One DataView witness algorithm computes the current data, offset and extent for fixed and length-tracking views, including out-of-bounds recovery after resize. Collector-owned byte snapshots keep native borrows valid across resize and detachment; native weak-link checks prove that released snapshots become collectible. Node differential properties cover ordinary metadata and receivers, options, conversions, species, transfers and view transitions, with one deep retained buffer/view graph. Fully typed ArrayBuffer, DataView and stdio consumers pass in both memory modes. See tests/property/arraybuffer-owner.property.test.ts, tests/property/arraybuffer-storage-gc.property.test.ts and tests/e2e/cases/array_buffer_ordinary. SharedArrayBuffer/Atomics, complete TypedArray integration, cross-realm semantics and exhaustive eligible Test262 evidence remain open; this does not establish full target conformance.

DataView now has per-realm ordinary constructor/prototype owners. One element/getter collection creates its public functions and descriptors; a shared byte-access algorithm validates private receiver slots, performs ordered index and Number/BigInt conversions, checks attachment and bounds, and reads or writes the requested byte order. Binary16 conversion rounds directly from binary64, and shared GMP projections preserve complete 64-bit BigInt patterns. Constructors retain the initial bounds snapshot across length conversion, read the actual newTarget prototype, and recheck attachment before publication. The old DataView CType, constructor/property lowering and name-based effect shortcuts have been removed.

Node differential properties cover metadata, mutable owners/accessors, borrowed and Proxy receivers, complete argument Lists, scalar byte formats and conversion/detachment ordering, with one deep retained view/buffer graph under collection. Fully typed DataView consumers and ArrayBuffer and stdio integration regressions run in both memory modes. Buffer.byteLength reads a view's public byteLength property and preserves boxed getter results. The shared numeric buffer-index kernel now truncates finite inputs, maps NaN to zero, and throws RangeError for invalid indices. Shared backing stores, complete TypedArray integration, cross-realm behavior and exhaustive eligible Test262 evidence remain open; these checks do not establish full ECMAScript conformance.

Stdio write calls evaluate one complete boxed argument List before runtime validation and schedule callable completions on the shared next-tick worklist. Each stream retains its current batching record; equal callbacks share one job, successful callbacks receive one null argument, and recursive writes enqueue through the same growing collection. Non-callable callbacks are ignored. The compiler recognizes only unshadowed process bindings or process imports and uses this path from both TypeScript and JavaScript. Consumed next-tick slots release their retained environments; the artificial recursion-depth guard has been removed. Function-body scope initialization now unwinds its compiler stacks when initialization reports a diagnostic.

Node differential properties cover callback timing, batching, arguments, receiver semantics, validation, local process shadowing, and one deep retained callback graph across CLI/native observation and both memory modes. Typed stdio and recursive next-tick E2Es and native weak-lifetime checks provide regression evidence. Ordinary process/stdio owners, the complete Writable end/backpressure/error surface, all byte codecs and typed arrays remain open. These local checks do not establish full ECMAScript conformance.

Buffer static calls now lower through one complete boxed argument List and canonical operation collection. Native result projections come from the same collection, preserving existing byte and numeric API boundaries. Runtime validation follows evaluation of every argument, so computed numeric inputs and spread/extra arguments retain their source semantics. Concatenation keeps the source Array authoritative across validation and copy passes, including changing getters and Proxy reads, and uses the shared relational operation for loop bounds. The fixed compiler static cases and native pointer-array concatenation predecessors were deleted. Independent Node properties cover semantic partitions and one deep retained byte-owner graph; the original filesystem abort allocation trigger passes in both memory modes. Mutable Buffer static owners/constructor identity, complete byte codecs and general Uint8Array/TypedArray ownership remain open, as does full target conformance.

Buffer verification: independent Node comparisons passed for runtime argument validation, argument/conversion order, changing concatenation sources, private byte-view lengths and one deep retained graph across typed and JavaScript CLI/native observation runs in both memory modes. The typed static-argument regression, original Buffer fixture and filesystem allocation trigger passed; descriptor-derived result projection also restores native transcode integration. Stdio Buffer calls compile again, but synchronous write-callback delivery was independently reproduced at the committed baseline and remains a separate host gap. Build, compliance types and the structural matrix check passed. Pinned witnesses remain diagnostic without a conformance promotion.

Error instances now use ordinary property owners. One canonical field collection and shared setter retain decoded payload roots before publication and transfer ownership to ordinary descriptors; mutation releases replaced payloads. The compiler no longer uses a native Error CType or fixed property/method lowering. AggregateError consumes the shared IteratorRecord after message conversion and cause lookup. Independent Node comparisons cover presence, descriptors, mutation, receiver checks, coercion/access order, derived owners and one deep retained graph through suspension and collection; direct collector tests prove retention and release before and after publication. Full target conformance and Node-specific stack/console inspection remain open. The broad pending-return fixture also has stale filesystem tags/timer ordering and an absent text-codec result requiring separate investigation.

Error verification: independent Node comparisons passed for the ordinary Error property and iterator partitions, suspension and a deep retained graph in typed and JavaScript CLI/native observation runs with both memory modes. The native collector proof also checks publication, replacement, owner release and diagnostics that do not invoke getters. Focused Error E2Es passed in both memory modes. The shared Buffer static path now repairs the boxed numeric allocation boundary reproduced at the committed baseline; Node timer abort wrapping and stack inspection remain separate host gaps. Pinned cause/property-descriptor witnesses remain diagnostic evidence without a conformance promotion.

Crypto owner verification: independent Node comparisons cover algorithm lookup, byte input and finalization, direct constructor/prototype/field/symbol/method descriptors, borrowed/Proxy receivers, mutation, copy, original import identities, default export mutation, Node 26 constructor wrapper metadata and one deep retained-owner graph under collection. Typed and JavaScript CLI/native observation runs pass in both memory modes. Fully typed imported/suspended consumers and crypto E2Es pass in both modes; ArrayBuffer/view regressions, source/compliance builds, threaded syntax and the matrix check pass. Frozen pinned symbol-descriptor and own-symbol invariant reports (/tmp/tsc2c-crypto154-final2-descriptors.json and /tmp/tsc2c-crypto154-final2-symbols.json) pass natively and have independent unchanged source/index/implementation, exact modern metadata, corpus/report and runner-owned native artifact/containment audits. These are scoped diagnostic witnesses on an ineligible host and do not promote a full clause. The pending-return fixture now compiles; its Node oracle confirms stale filesystem tags and early ordering, while native AggregateError cause retention still differs. Crypto stream/module parity, deprecation delivery, complete codecs/options/providers and full target conformance remain open.

Crypto Hash and HMAC core operations now share ordinary value owners, a per-Realm descriptor collection, one digest-state lifecycle and generic JavaScript calls. The native pointer CTypes and specialized update/digest lowering have been removed. Dynamic OpenSSL algorithm lookup replaces fixed algorithm chains; provider enumeration filters algorithms that initialize successfully. String inputs use well-formed UTF-8, Buffer/DataView inputs project byte storage, and ArrayBuffer allocation explicitly zeroes its backing store, default and unknown output encodings return Buffers, Hash copy has independent state, and finalized-state checks precede handle access. Core constructor/factory imports retain original namespace identities alongside mutable default exports, including Node 26 exported constructor wrapper metadata. Broader crypto module ownership, deprecation warning delivery, Node stream prototype ancestry/behavior, TypedArray/KeyObject/ArrayBuffer key parity, XOF/cSHAKE options, complete codecs/error metadata and exact Node provider inventory remain open; unrepresented digest options/codecs fail with one stable diagnostic. Full target conformance is not established.

Dirent verification: independent Node constructor/prototype/field/symbol/receiver/kind/import/mutation and suspended-call partitions agree across typed/JavaScript CLI/native observation and both memory modes, including one deep linked owner graph under collection. Fully typed imported consumers, Node-derived descriptor/parentPath fixture expectations and final Dirent E2Es pass in both modes; asynchronous readdir and directory-handle transport/traversal regressions pass in their complementary GC/no-GC runs. The shared URL boundary property, source/compliance builds, threaded runtime syntax and matrix check pass. Frozen pinned own-symbol-key invariant and builtin-method construction reports (`/tmp/tsc2c-dirent153-final1-symbols.json`, `/tmp/tsc2c-dirent153-final1-method.json`) pass natively with independently audited unchanged source/implementation, exact modern metadata, corpus/report and runner-owned artifact/containment attestations. These are scoped diagnostic witnesses on an ineligible host, without promoting a full clause. Full filesystem ownership, Stats parity, HMAC boxing and complete target conformance remain open.

Dirent result owners and value-array transport are implemented. Final source/memory properties, shared-boundary regressions and pinned native audits are verified for this checkpoint. At the Dirent checkpoint the pending-return regression stopped at HMAC boxing; the crypto owner checkpoint removes that compile boundary and reaches an open stdout ordering mismatch; full filesystem module/API ownership and Stats parity remain open.

Weak-lifetime verification: independent Node metadata, weak-key, validation-order, derived/retained-owner, callable-proxy and unregistration partitions agree with typed/JavaScript CLI/native observation in both memory modes, including one deep retained-owner input. Native weak links prove constructor and deref job retention, weak target/token release, pending unregistration and holdings through deferred cleanup; inactive C test frames are scrubbed for both lifetime and ephemeron-cycle proofs. Shared ephemeron and canonical class constructor/heritage partitions, typed imported E2Es, Node fixture comparisons, both TypeScript builds, threaded runtime syntax and the matrix check pass. Frozen pinned WeakRef symbol-target construction and FinalizationRegistry symbol-token unregistration reports (`/tmp/tsc2c-weak152-final1-ref.json`, `/tmp/tsc2c-weak152-final1-registry.json`) pass natively with independent unchanged-source, exact modern metadata, corpus/report and runner-owned artifact/containment audits. This remains scoped evidence on an ineligible host; complete managing-memory conformance and the immutable full E2E runs remain open.

Weak-lifetime owners and native job/collector behavior are implemented; complete eligible managing-memory conformance and the immutable complete E2E runs before promoting coverage. The pending-return regression now advances through WeakRef/FinalizationRegistry and stops at fs.Dirent boxing; that shared owner remains open.

Weak-collection verification: independent Node operation, metadata, iterator, weak-key, reentrant-computation, callable-proxy, derived-construction and retained-call partitions agree with typed/JavaScript CLI/native-observation execution in both memory modes. One deep ephemeron graph passes; the native weak-link check verifies reachable-key retention and unreachable-cycle release with the setup outside the collector-visible caller frame. Fully typed imported consumers, dynamic weak-key regressions and corrected empty/invalid source assertions pass in GC/no-GC modes and agree with Node. Shared strong-collection/ordinary-Get/global-owner properties, source/compliance builds, threaded collector syntax and coverage-reference checks pass. Final pinned WeakMap computed-insertion and WeakSet symbol-iterable metadata variants pass; both independent source, implementation, containment, corpus, report and native-artifact audits completed successfully before this note. The broader retained-return regression advances to its separate unowned FinalizationRegistry. This remains scoped evidence on an ineligible host; eligible exhaustive target conformance is not complete.

WeakMap and WeakSet now use ordinary per-Realm constructor/prototype owners and one encoded entry representation. A collector dependency index connects entries to both collection and key, and one heap worklist closes newly eligible value graphs before dead keys are cleared. Normal/atomic native allocation kinds are traced; unsupported collector allocations fail closed with one diagnostic. Node semantic partitions and one deep ephemeron chain cover typed/JavaScript CLI and native-observation execution in both memory modes. A native weak-link check separately verifies reachable-key retention and release of an unreachable key/value cycle. Source weak CType, array-only constructor and direct method predecessors are removed. The declared surface admits object/non-registered-symbol weak keys, generic iterables and the target getOrInsert/getOrInsertComputed methods; invalid inserted keys raise ordinary TypeErrors. No new CType is needed. The fully typed imported consumer is weak_collection_shared_owner. The original retained-return regression now advances to an unowned FinalizationRegistry; WeakRef/finalization lifetime and job semantics and eligible full target coverage remain open.

Console-owner verification: independent Node observations agree with typed/JavaScript CLI and native-observation execution in both memory modes, including retained calls, stream selection, formatting partitions and one deep owner/actual-List stress input. Console regressions, the existing nested-expression consumer, shared canonical-global and URL-owner properties, and retained GC executions pass. URL regressions pass apart from the old implicit-console string expectation; its corrected explicit String(url) assertion independently agrees with Node and passes in both modes. The optional libdispatch case remains skipped where the library is absent. Source/compliance builds and coverage-reference checks pass. The final pinned Function.bind length and Reflect.get non-constructor metadata variants pass, and both independent source, implementation, containment, corpus, report and native-artifact audits finished successfully before this note. These are scoped diagnostics on an ineligible host; full target coverage remains open.

The URL parsing fixture now calls String(url) explicitly for its string-conversion assertion; Node console inspection of specialized URL objects remains part of the documented inspection gap.

Console integration exposed a GC lifetime regression in retained URL construction. URL constructor results now keep a decoded owner root across newTarget prototype lookup; member publication also anchors callable/descriptor temporaries, and the shared object allocator anchors an encoded prototype across allocation. The descriptor-inspection worklist retains decoded value and descriptor roots. These repairs preserve ordinary ownership rather than adding source-shape cases.

Console globals and console/node:console imports now share ordinary per-Realm owners, bound method identities and original module export cells. One member collection drives runtime publication and source admission; ordinary References and actual Lists retain methods through suspension and accept spread/rest calls without arity cases. The old direct console emitter is removed. A descriptor worklist formats arrays and objects without invoking accessors, and formatting preserves primitive conversions, ignored styles, unpaired-surrogate replacement and stdout/stderr selection. Native-observation stdout capture uses the same writer. Independent Node semantic partitions and one deep retained-owner/argument stress input cover typed/JavaScript CLI and native profiles in both memory modes; the fully typed imported consumer is console_shared_source_owner. No new CType or declaration is needed because the declared Console surface already contains these methods. Complete Node inspection, Console construction/additional APIs and full target conformance remain open.

URL-owner verification: independent Node 26 observations agree with typed/JavaScript CLI and native-observation properties in GC/no-GC modes, including one retained owner graph. URL regression runs and the typed imported/file-URL consumers pass in both memory modes; the optional libdispatch case remains skipped where the library is absent. A reproduced optimized GC payload-reclamation fault was repaired in the shared native-object boxing helper, and retained executions plus source-callable/global-owner properties pass. The exact Reflect.get/not-a-constructor pin passes its source-metadata variants, and its independent source, implementation, containment, corpus, report and native-artifact audit finished successfully before this note. This remains scoped diagnostic evidence on an ineligible host; full target coverage is not complete.

URL and URLSearchParams now use ordinary per-Realm constructors, member descriptors and cached payload owners. Global and node:url references share the canonical actual-List and Reference lowering; imported exports enter the existing retained import-cell collection. One live parameter list drives constructor input, iteration, forEach and URL query synchronization. Old direct URL construction, method/property lowering and purity assumptions are removed. Node semantic partitions, one deep retained-owner graph and typed imported consumers exercise this host extension. The filesystem tagged path boundary publishes Stats members, while complete Stats/native-boxed parity, filesystem default Buffer results, complete WHATWG parsing and other ambient owners such as console remain open. No ECMAScript clause is promoted and full target coverage is not complete.

Expression CFG regressions now retain the shared `tsc_async_cfg_` and completion/capture checks without requiring the obsolete `expression_value_` predecessor name. The existing runtime assertions and expected observations are unchanged. Independent Node execution and native GC/no-GC E2E runs verify the expression completion/condition behavior and adjacent sequence/spread regression. This is a regression-expectation repair, not language-coverage promotion; full target coverage remains open and the immutable full-suite runs continue.

The physical source-value fact pass now marks unchecked JavaScript reads, calls and construction from their canonical tagged Reference/actual-List boundaries before checker inference. The same worklist retains callee, owner, mutation and alias dependencies; unchecked property/element References follow their containing owner without inferring a receiver property symbol. Redundant source-formal signature edges are removed because all executable source formals already have canonical actual-value storage. This repairs the recursive inference failure in the existing source-flow stress input without a depth limit, larger checker stack or fixture-count special case. Generated Node semantic partitions and the retained 4096-cell alias graph now compile and execute in both memory modes; adjacent CLI/native eval and typed callable/storage checks guard the shared policy. Empty native observation plans no longer activate typed eval environments: activation follows represented eval records or discovered invocations, preserving native class layouts in Modules without eval. Full target coverage and arbitrary runtime-source/complete Realm evaluation remain open.

Verification checkpoint: source/compliance builds and matrix/checklist validation pass. The existing generated source-flow semantic partitions and extended single 4096-cell alias stress input pass in actual GC/no-GC modes, including late property/element reads and eval. Independent Node CLI/native eval checks, typed/erased callable-formal and uninitialized-storage properties, Module eval/context checks and imported callable/storage E2E consumers pass. The final pinned lexical-environment eval observation passes its metadata-derived variants; its independent source/implementation/containment/corpus/report/native-artifact audit completed successfully before this note. This remains scoped diagnostic evidence on an ineligible host. The full-suite snapshot runs are still active and full target coverage remains open.

CLI and native-host eval admission now project the same canonical source-value graph into one finite source worklist, closed together with Function-constructor records. Direct, indirect and host Script requests retain source text, caller strictness and effective strictness as separate identity fields; generated roots preserve UTF-16 code units. Ordinary CLI eval uses the same retained lexical/private environments and exact-source dispatch as native observation, including nested eval/Function discovery, non-string inputs and runtime SyntaxError completions. The original Realm eval intrinsic comes from the canonical global-function descriptor collection, remains nonconstructible, and direct-eval identity checks survive global-property replacement. The old constant-only eval emitter and separate manifest result dispatcher have been removed; manifests feed the shared source collection and unresolved sources retain one diagnostic or the explicit unsafe bridge. Independent Node semantic partitions cover Script/Module strictness, live closures, private brands, identity/mutation, source validity and UTF-16 identity, with one retained deep owner graph across CLI/native hosts and both memory modes. Imported typed consumers and updated finite-source regression expectations provide adjacent evidence. Arbitrary runtime source compilation and complete eval/newTarget/super/Realm partitions remain open. The source-flow alias stress failure was subsequently repaired by the shared fact-pass change described above; aggregate fixture results do not establish full target coverage.

Verification checkpoint: source and compliance builds, matrix/checklist validation, the independent Node Script/Module and CLI/native-host eval properties in both memory modes, retained private-eval/cell properties, the canonical global-owner regression, imported typed consumers in actual GC/no-GC modes and the updated finite-eval/manifest regressions pass. Final pinned `test/built-ins/eval/name.js`, `test/built-ins/eval/no-construct.js` and `private-field-visible-to-direct-eval.js` observations pass their metadata-derived variants. All independent source/implementation/containment/corpus/report/artifact audits finished successfully before this note. The eval pins support scoped diagnostic evidence on the ineligible host; the legacy private anchor remains unmapped. The immutable feature-143 full-suite runs remain active, and full target coverage is not complete.

Admitted direct eval source records now validate external private names against the caller environment before executing any source statement. The canonical class-name collection projects visible identities, including shadowing and heritage boundaries, into a separate private-name collection on the retained Environment Record tree. Ordinary binding lookup cannot observe or replace that collection. Eval-created functions retain the same identities; missing names fail with SyntaxError and wrong receiver brands still fail with TypeError. Static validation admits external names only for identified direct-eval roots and preserves all other private grammar checks. Compact independent Node properties cover source reuse across different private environments, fields/accessors/methods, initialization, nested eval, retained closures and suspension in both memory modes with one deep owner graph. Ordinary CLI admission of nonconstant eval source, complete eval context rules and eligible full target evidence remain open.

Verification: source/compliance builds and runtime C syntax checks pass. Independent Node differential native-observation properties pass in both memory modes with the retained depth stress input; ordinary private static semantics and retained direct-eval cell neighbors also pass. Final pinned method/initializer private-eval tests pass, with independent frozen-source metadata, corpus/report/implementation digests, containment and native artifact audits. Their sec-privatefieldget source anchor remains unmapped on the ineligible host. This evidence covers admitted eval records and does not claim ordinary CLI nonconstant eval admission or full target compliance.

Member-only decorated TypeScript classes now publish the original constructor through the existing constructor owner. Constructor replacement cells are read only when the class itself has decorators, matching their allocation and initialization condition. This repairs the current native compile failure for decorated accessors and imported constructor identity; it changes no decorator application ordering and makes no ECMAScript clause claim.

Verification: source/compliance builds, matrix validation, the original accessor regression in both actual memory modes, the class replacement-order neighbor and imported alias/constructor-identity E2E all pass. The imported E2E matches independent Node execution after TypeScript emits its decorator helper. The existing mixed static/instance accessor fixture still uses project-specific application order; Node TypeScript-emitted code applies static accessors first, so complete decorator ordering remains open extension work. No Test262 clause evidence is inferred from this TypeScript syntax fix.

Continuation/Reflect release observations now use the canonical CFG path and specified exception constructor. The obsolete expression_value_4 generated-field assertion is removed because that fixed-shape emitter no longer exists; the behavioral E2E remains. Reflect rejection observation preserves arbitrary rejection values and identifies TypeError independently of its message. Both regressions match independent Node output and pass with GC and TSC2C_NO_GC=1. These are regression-detector repairs, with no production or clause-coverage change.

Array exception regressions now check the specified TypeError or RangeError constructor instead of obsolete implementation-specific messages. Independent Node execution confirms all non-error observations remain unchanged, including sparse mutation and writable-length behavior. The selected E2E regressions pass with GC and with the harness actual TSC2C_NO_GC=1 setting. This repairs the release detectors after the canonical array algorithms; it changes no production semantics or compliance status. The imported private_class_elements E2E also passes with that actual no-GC setting, confirming its earlier differential property evidence.

Private class elements now use one lexical name collection, fresh identities retained by each evaluated class constructor, and a separate runtime PrivateElement collection. The shared ordered ClassElement List installs private method/accessor brands before field initializers; ordinary, destructuring and resumed References retain the same receiver and name identity for get/set/brand checks. Getter/setter pairs share an identity, nested heritage uses the outer private environment, and private fields remain writable on frozen receivers in the selected nonbrowser profile. The same collection validates private-name early errors before code generation, including duplicate declarations, unresolved names, deletion and super access. This closes the represented private-element path; direct eval/private-environment integration, remaining class semantics and eligible full target coverage remain open.

Verification: source/compliance builds, independent Node differential CLI/native-observation checks in both memory modes with one deep retained owner graph, generated private-name static-semantic partitions, imported typed E2E in both memory modes with an independent Node comparison, the public resumed-class neighbor and the continuation model checks all pass. Final pinned private-method immutability and duplicate-brand tests pass at exact current anchors; the public/private-name collision and static/instance accessor early-error tests also pass but retain their unmapped legacy/pending source anchors. Independent metadata-aware audits validate the frozen source, corpus/report digests, implementation manifest, containment and native/compiler transcripts. These remain diagnostic records on an ineligible host, not a full target claim.

Admitted TypeScript enum member constants now reach the existing ordered member projection before runtime receiver lowering. This restores scalar reads in functions, methods and resumptions when source-value analysis marks their surrounding expressions as canonical values; no nonexistent enum receiver is emitted. Compact independent Node differential properties and the original selector regression supply local extension evidence. General runtime enum objects and full ECMAScript target coverage remain open.

Verification: source/compliance builds, independent TypeScript-erased Node differential CLI/native-observation properties in both memory modes with the retained depth stress graph, the original selector E2E regression in both memory modes and an independent Node comparison, the resumable-expression model, and generated coverage-reference checks pass. This is TypeScript extension evidence; no Test262 or exact ECMAScript clause promotion is inferred.

Host base64 function references now resolve through the canonical Realm global function collection instead of seeking a module owner for ambient declarations. Shared function descriptors record construction, enumerability and required arguments, while the existing actual-argument list supplies calls, apply/bind/spread and retained suspension. The obsolete unqualified direct-call path is removed; qualified Buffer interop remains separate. This fixes the observed suspended-callee regression. Existing codec byte/error semantics are unchanged and no ECMAScript clause is promoted by this host-extension evidence. Full target coverage remains open.

Verification: source/compliance builds, the independent Node owner property with its retained depth stress input, imported inferred typed E2E in both memory modes and an independent Node comparison, the previously failing suspended-callee regression in both memory modes, the neighboring canonical global owner property, and generated coverage-reference checks pass. Standard parseInt descriptor/construction selections pass natively with independently audited source fingerprints, exact strict/sloppy metadata and runner-owned artifacts. These probes verify preservation of standard function records, not base64 codec conformance. The host remains ineligible.

Module evaluation now uses one retained Module Record graph for synchronous and top-level-await bodies. An iterative DFS/SCC worklist preserves requested-module order, async dependency edges, rejection identity and shared completion capabilities. Awaiting bodies use the common continuation graph and existing Module Environment binding cells; dynamic import waits for evaluation and resolves the actual namespace, including its then export. Anonymous default classes in typed awaiting modules use the same canonical binding preparation. HasTLA visits definition-time class and object-method keys while excluding retained bodies. The fixed synchronous ES-module evaluation cache is removed; the CommonJS host wrapper remains separate. Complete exact-clause certification, host eligibility, private elements and full eval environments remain open.

Verification: source/compliance builds, the independent Node Module graph property with the retained depth stress input, imported typed E2E in both memory modes and an independent Node comparison, existing linking/declaration/namespace/dynamic-import properties, continuation/class/CommonJS neighbors, and generated coverage-reference checks pass. Native top-level-await class-key, DFS parent-order and independent sibling-evaluation selections pass with independently audited unchanged source fingerprints, exact module metadata and runner-owned artifacts. The host remains ineligible and whole target coverage remains open.

Class definitions now lower through one ordered stage plan shared by ordinary evaluation, native member definition and the continuation worklist. Heritage and computed element keys are definition-time expressions; retained constructor, method and initializer bodies remain separate. Async functions, generators and async generators preserve the constructor, actual property keys and immutable inner name through these stages, including abrupt/abandoned definitions. Dynamic inner-name value/root/TDZ storage belongs to the retained constructor rather than separate temporary allocations. The obsolete suspension diagnostic fixture is removed; resumable_class_definition_stages supplies imported typed consumers, and the independent resumable-class-definition property covers semantic partitions and one deep retained owner graph in both memory modes. Top-level awaiting module evaluation, private elements, complete eval environments and eligible full target coverage remain open.

JavaScript class declarations now use the same ClassDefinitionEvaluation factory as expressions and local declarations. Script lexical cells, ES module live bindings, anonymous default exports and CommonJS bindings retain the canonical constructor value; JavaScript declarations no longer emit parallel native structs, constructor adapters or member bodies. A worklist over superclass dependencies also routes TypeScript subclasses of these classes through the same factory. Construction aliases and instanceof use that value without native casts.
